# Repository Memory

This file is durable context for agents working in this repository. Read it when starting work, after context compression, or when unsure about the current checkpoint, validation policy, or parked debt. Update it with concise facts that future agents should remember.

## Memory Rules

- Record durable decisions, accepted/rejected behavior, current best checkpoints, validation baselines, and parked debt.
- Do not record PDF payloads, Base64, large logs, generated benchmark JSON, or transient command output.
- Prefer exact stage numbers, run directories, commit hashes, and one-line conclusions.
- If a fact is uncertain, label it as uncertain and point to the local artifact that should be checked.

Result: `351` processed, mean `92.4387`, median `95`, grades `323 A / 4 B / 2 C / 9 D / 9 F / 4 ?`, rows below `93`: `36`, points needed `197`, runtime p95/max `212965ms / 300020ms`, and `false_positive_applied=0`. Hard timeouts were `0114`, `0019/long-4516`, `0031/structure-4438`, and `0208/4446`. R42 confirms that fresh full-run closure is currently blocked by runtime/analyzer volatility and low-score route drops, not by missing virtual overlay arithmetic; next useful work should target general hard-timeout recovery or repeatable low-row structural signatures before another fresh 351-PDF run.
- Stage200 low-score orphan-remap guard was tested and reverted/not kept on 2026-05-15. The guard rejected low-score (`<=59`) no-gain stages where `remap_orphan_mcids_as_artifacts` applied without invariant-backed structural benefit; target evidence was promising (`run-stage200-low-score-orphan-remap-guard-target-2026-05-15-r1` recovered `0114/4587 30/F -> 96/A`, repeat `run-stage200-low-score-orphan-remap-guard-0114-repeat-2026-05-15-r1` recovered `30/F -> 93/A`, both `false_positive_applied=0`). Affected shard validation failed to reproduce the hard-timeout recovery: `Output/goal-all-input-mean-2026-05-09-r1/fresh-all-input-validation-2026-05-15-r43-stage200-shard02/shard-02` left `0114` as `0/? per_pdf_timeout_300000ms`, shard mean `92.8182` versus r42 shard `93.0000`, points needed unchanged at `117`, and `false_positive_applied=0`. Commit `129612a` was reverted by `9d6ac37`; treat Stage200 as diagnostic-only and do not reintroduce it without a new affected-context proof.
- Stage201 MCID resolved-text ordering was tested and reverted/not kept on 2026-05-15. Diagnostic analysis on `0208/4446` showed a real analyzer issue: bad raw repeats had `paragraphStructElems=0` and MCID text attached to the wrong marked-content block (`WOMEN AND REENTRY` resolving as `John Maki`), preventing `create_heading_from_tagged_visible_anchor`; a temporary `extract_resolved_text_after_mcid` fix bounded lookup to the current `EMC` block and selected the earliest text operator, making bad repeats schedule the existing tagged-visible heading route. Target/control validation `Output/goal-all-input-mean-2026-05-09-r1/run-stage201-mcid-text-resolver-target-2026-05-15-r1` rejected the source change for now: `0208` still hard-timed out (`0/? per_pdf_timeout_300000ms`), `0114` hard-timed out, `0019/long-4516` reached `87/B`, `0135` stayed `69/D`, and `false_positive_applied=0`; one `0242` control symlink was invalid and should be ignored. Do not reintroduce the resolver behavior unless a follow-up proves it removes a timeout or preserves broad controls; the observed MCID text attribution bug remains a useful analyzer diagnostic.
- Stage202/204 timeout-checkpoint follow-up was diagnostic-only; no source behavior was kept. A one-off trace harness for `0208/4446` at `Output/goal-all-input-mean-2026-05-09-r1/stage202-timeout-trace-0208-2026-05-15-r1/0208-trace.json` completed `96/A` in about `176s`, confirming the existing route is sufficient when the good analyzer path reproduces. `0019/long-4516` raw analysis without the Stage201 MCID patch repeatedly stayed on the bad `43/F` shape; the temporary MCID patch produced a `58/F` shape once but was volatile and remains rejected. A temporary low-score timeout checkpoint experiment allowing `>=60`/`+8` D-grade near-wall returns was also rejected: `0120/4690` completed `65/D` under the new rule, but the counterfactual old-rule run `Output/goal-all-input-mean-2026-05-09-r1/run-stage204-counterfactual-0120-oldrule-2026-05-15-r1` also completed `64/D`, so the source change was not causal. Stage204 target/control output `Output/goal-all-input-mean-2026-05-09-r1/run-stage204-dgrade-low-score-checkpoint-2026-05-15-r1` had `0019`, `0031`, and `0135` still timing out, `0114 93/A`, `0120 65/D`, and `false_positive_applied=0`; treat this as runtime/analyzer volatility evidence, not accepted movement.
- The team accepted r39 as the new practical all-input baseline on 2026-05-15 with an explicit one-raw-point waiver from the former strict `>=93.0000` target. Baseline artifact: `Output/goal-all-input-mean-2026-05-09-r1/fresh-all-input-validation-diagnostic-2026-05-14-r39-stage194-lowconcurrency-full-r1/all-input-mean-diagnostic.json`; it is a fresh merged all-unique-PDF validation over `351` PDFs, not a spot check or virtual remix. Accepted metrics: mean `92.9972`, median `95`, grades `327 A / 6 B / 1 C / 6 D / 7 F / 4 ?`, rows below `93`: `33`, points needed for strict `93.0000`: `1`, runtime p95/max `197611ms / 300016ms`, and `false_positive_applied=0`. Treat this as the baseline for future comparison; do not claim the measured mean was literally `93.0000`, and do not let later partial r44 shards supersede it without a completed all-unique diagnostic. A partially started r44 validation completed only shards 01-04 before user interruption and is not the accepted baseline.
- Docker API rebuild from commit `c74e5bd` produced local image `pdfaf-v2:local` id `sha256:b179b2c4c35ebdaf134e03ef3b737bef31c1cecef78a11f3ab58d748c6942030` on 2026-05-16 and the compose API container was healthy with embedded LLM reachable. Verification artifact `Output/docker-api-parity-2026-05-16-r1/results/api-parity-summary.json` compared source API on port `6300` against Docker API on port `6200` with semantic options explicitly disabled. Stable rows matched on final score for `2023-focus.pdf` (`95/A`) and `v1-4758` (`92/A`), and all checked rows had `false_positive_applied=0`; however exact parity failed on `pdfaf_fixture_inaccessible.pdf` (`55/F` source vs `54/F` Docker) and volatile all-input row `0120/4690` (`65/D` source vs `90/A` Docker, Docker repeat `77/C` in `docker-repeat-summary.json`). Do not push this image as a verified parity update unless the team accepts the remaining runtime/analyzer variance or a narrower stable-only Docker validation is substituted.
- Root cause for the 2026-05-16 Docker/source scoring mismatch is environment/analyzer drift, not different source code. The Docker image used unpinned `pip3 install pikepdf fonttools 'ocrmypdf>=17.4,<18'`, which resolved to Python `3.13.5`, `pikepdf 10.6.0`, `fonttools 4.63.0`, and `ocrmypdf 17.4.2`; the host source engine used Python `3.13.7`, `pikepdf 10.5.1`, `fonttools 4.62.1`, and distro `ocrmypdf 16.10.4`. On the same `0120/4690` PDF and identical `python/pdf_analysis_helper.py`, raw helper output changed from host-style ~`1890` paragraph struct elems / `4` headings / `39` figures to Docker `1179` paragraph struct elems / `3` headings / `37` figures; temporarily downgrading the running container to `pikepdf 10.5.1` and `fonttools 4.62.1` restored paragraph counts near host levels, proving the Dockerfile dependency drift is causal. Even with matching package versions the helper still showed repeat variance on `4690`, so exact API parity also requires addressing existing structural analyzer nondeterminism and avoiding learned-DB/playbook state in parity checks. The running container packages were restored to the image-resolved versions after the diagnostic; no Docker Hub push was made.
- Docker API parity rebuild `pdfaf-v2:local` id `sha256:32854a67da49bcdb831b37c7f38355103e60b77a9117139692a2d99f84411193` pins Docker analyzer deps to `pikepdf 10.5.1`, `fonttools 4.62.1`, and `ocrmypdf 17.4.2`. Verification artifact `Output/docker-api-parity-2026-05-16-r2/results/api-parity-summary.json` compared source API on port `6300` against Docker API on port `6200` with semantic options explicitly disabled and copied DB state. Stable rows now match on before/final score and false-positive truth: `pdfaf_fixture_inaccessible 40/F -> 55/F`, `2023-focus 50/F -> 95/A`, and `v1-4758 59/F -> 92/A`, all with `false_positive_applied=0`. Volatile row `0120/4690` now matches final score (`69/D`) with `false_positive_applied=0`, but still differed on initial analyzer score (`54/F` source vs `53/F` Docker) and tool route because the known structural analyzer nondeterminism remains. Treat the Docker package pin as accepted for Docker/source packaging parity; do not claim exact per-tool determinism on `4690` until analyzer nondeterminism is fixed.
- Docker Hub API tags were updated for the pinned-analyzer rebuild from commit `88efbf6`: `hendo420/pdfaf-v2:all-input-r39-pinned-analyzer-2026-05-16` and `hendo420/pdfaf-v2:latest`, both at digest `sha256:32854a67da49bcdb831b37c7f38355103e60b77a9117139692a2d99f84411193`. - Colorado ORS PPP follow-up accepted a general bounded Stage 180 table/header regularization sequence on 2026-05-18. The source change is structure-based, not source/PDF-specific: native/tagged PDFs with low table markup, stable non-table categories, no direct/misplaced table shape, bounded header-association debt, and remaining irregular-row evidence can run same-handle `set_table_header_cells` plus bounded `normalize_table_structure` passes to avoid stale table refs. Candidate run `/mnt/pdf-review/pdfaf-validation/colorado-ors-ppp-stage180-candidate-2026-05-18-r1/baseline_report.json` processed the 20 Colorado PDFs with mean `63.70 -> 93.65`, median `95`, `false_positive_applied=0`, p95/max `168075ms/200375ms`, and lifted the 2012 row `69->95`, 2011 `69->88`, and 2013 `88->92`. Public PDFs and public-source artifacts from this source set should be deleted after metrics extraction.
